=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
Whether a non-empty sequence of non-negative integers (V_1, V_2, \dots, V_M) is Polish or not is recursively defined as follows:

- We say (V_1, V_2, \dots, V_M) is Polish if there exist V_1 Polish sequences W_1, W_2, \dots, W_{V_1} such that the concatenation of sequences (V_1), W_1, W_2, \dots, W_{V_1} in this order equals (V_1, V_2, \dots, V_M).

In particular, the sequence (0) is Polish.
Given a sequence of non-negative integers (A_1, A_2, \dots, A_N) of length N, find the number of Polish sequences of length N that are lexicographically not greater than (A_1, A_2, \dots, A_N), modulo 998244353.
 What is lexicographical order on sequences?
We say that sequence S = (S_1,S_2,\ldots,S_{|S|}) is lexicographically less than sequence T = (T_1,T_2,\ldots,T_{|T|}) if either condition 1. or 2. below holds.
Here, |S|, |T| represent the lengths of S, T respectively.

-  |S| \lt |T| and (S_1,S_2,\ldots,S_{|S|}) = (T_1,T_2,\ldots,T_{|S|}). 
-  There exists an integer 1 \leq i \leq \min\lbrace |S|, |T| \rbrace such that both of the following hold:

-  (S_1,S_2,\ldots,S_{i-1}) = (T_1,T_2,\ldots,T_{i-1})
-  S_i is (numerically) less than T_i.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
A_1 A_2 \dots A_N

Output

Print the number of sequences satisfying the conditions, modulo 998244353.

Constraints


- 1\leq N \leq 3\times 10^5
- 0\leq A_i \lt N
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

6
1 1 1 2 0 0

Sample Output 1

2

(1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 0) and (1, 1, 1, 2, 0, 0) satisfy the conditions.
We can verify that (1, 1, 1, 2, 0, 0) is Polish as follows.

- As stated in the problem statement, (0) is Polish.
- (2, 0, 0) is Polish because it equals the concatenation of (2) and two Polish sequences (0) and (0) in this order.
- (1, 2, 0, 0) is Polish because it equals the concatenation of (1) and one Polish sequence (2, 0, 0) in this order.
- (1, 1, 2, 0, 0) is Polish because it equals the concatenation of (1) and one Polish sequence (1, 2, 0, 0) in this order.
- (1, 1, 1, 2, 0, 0) is Polish because it equals the concatenation of (1) and one Polish sequence (1, 1, 2, 0, 0) in this order.

Sample Input 2

11
3 3 4 4 5 5 6 6 7 7 8

Sample Output 2

13002

Sample Input 3

19
18 18 18 18 18 18 18 18 18 18 18 18 18 18 18 18 18 18 18

Sample Output 3

477638700

Sample Input 4

4
1 1 0 0

Sample Output 4

0

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Now interval_sum function:
def range_sum(L, a, b):
    # sum_{m=a}^{b} F(L,m), with 1<=a<=b. F=0 if m>L.
    if a < 1: a=1
    if b < a: return 0
    n = L + 1
    # F(n, a+1) - F(n, b+2)
    return (F(n, a+1) - F(n, b+2)) % MOD
Need ensure if b maybe >L, F(n,b+2) zero if b+2 > n (b>L-1). If b=L, b+2=L+2>n=L+1 zero. Good. If a>L, then F(n,a+1) zero if a+1>n => a>L. Then result 0. But if a<=L and b>L, F(n,a+1) -0 = sum a..L, correct. If a<1? low max 1 so no.

MOD=998244353

Precompute fact and invfact:
maxM = 2*N + 5
fact = [1]*(maxM)
for i in range(1,maxM): fact[i]=fact[i-1]*i%MOD
invfact = [1]*maxM
invfact[-1]=pow(fact[-1], MOD-2, MOD)
for i in range(maxM-1,0,-1): invfact[i-1]=invfact[i]*i%MOD
def C(n,k):
    if k<0 or k>n or n<0: return 0
    return fact[n]*invfact[k]%MOD*invfact[n-k]%MOD

Need inv[n] for F formula. Precompute inv_num:
inv = [0]*(N+2)
for i in range(1,N+2): inv[i]=pow(i, MOD-2, MOD) would be O(N log) maybe okay but can do linear:
inv[1]=1
for i in range(2,N+2):
    inv[i] = MOD - MOD//i * inv[MOD%i] % MOD
Since MOD prime and i<MOD. Need up to N maybe N+1. Use N+5.

F(n,r):
if r < 1 or r > n: return 0
# if n <=0 return 0
return r * inv[n] % MOD * C(2*n - r - 1, n - 1) % MOD

Now range_sum:
def range_sum(L, a, b):
    if a < 1: a = 1
    if b < a: return 0
    n = L + 1
    return (F(n, a + 1) - F(n, b + 2)) % MOD
Need if b maybe huge > n? F handles. If a+1 maybe > n? F returns 0. If b+2 maybe > n? F returns 0. If a+1 maybe n+1 and b+2 maybe n+2, diff 0. Good.

def solve():
    import sys
    input=sys.stdin.readline
    N=int(input())
    A=list(map(int,input().split()))
    precompute...
    ans=0
    S=0
    broken=False
    for i in range(1,N): # 1-indexed i from 1 to N-1
        Ai=A[i-1]
        L=N-i
        low = S - i + 1
        if low < 1: low = 1
        high = S + Ai - i
        if high >= low:
            ans = (ans + range_sum(L, low, high)) % MOD
        S += Ai
        # check prefix A_i valid for i < N
        if S < i or S > N-1:
            broken=True
            break
    if not broken:
        ans = (ans + 1) % MOD
    print(ans%MOD)

Now, let's revisit the characterization of Polish sequences and F(L,m) to ensure no hidden issue: Definition says "there exist V1 Polish sequences W1..W_{V1}" such that concatenation (V1), W1..W_{V1} equals sequence. If V1=0, there exist 0 Polish sequences, concatenation is (0), so (0) Polish. This recursive definition allows a Polish sequence to have first value k and then exactly k Polish sequences. This is exactly plane trees with outdegree labels. The Łukasiewicz path condition: For a sequence of length N with sum N-1 and prefix sums >= k for k<N, is it sufficient? Let's derive continuation count F(L,m) more rigorously for own confidence.

Given prefix length i with D_i=d>=0. Let m=d+1. Remaining length L=N-i. We need count sequences C length L such that total sum = L-m (since final D=-1) and for j=1..L-1, D_{i+j}>=0. In terms of path H = D+1: start H_0=m, after L steps H_L=0, steps X_j=C_j-1>=-1, and H_j>=1 for j=1..L-1. This is exactly a Łukasiewicz path for a plane forest with L nodes and m roots? Standard: A plane forest with m roots and L nodes has preorder sequence of outdegrees C_j. The path starts at m, each node step outdegree-1, ends at 0, and stays positive before end. Number is F(L,m). Yes.

F(n,r) = r/(2n-r) * C(2n-r, n).
Using identity = r/n * C(2n-r-1, n-1). Let's prove:

Now, continuation length L=N-i. Need count F(L,m). Sum over m low..high. range_sum uses L. Good.

Now, update S += A_i. Prefix validity at i for A: need S >= i (for i<N). Also need S <= N-1? Actually if S > N-1, no valid completion, but prefix condition at i still holds. We break because no future valid. If S = N-1 and i<N, remaining sum zero, future values must be 0. Prefix condition for future: D_i = N-1-i = L-1. At next position j=i+1, if next value 0, P_{i+1}=N-1, D_{i+1}=N-1-(i+1)=L-2>=0 as long as i+1<N? If i=N-1, no next. Good. So S=N-1 valid. If S > N-1 break. If S < i break. Good.

But what if S > N-1 but i=N? Not loop. End condition only if not broken. If we somehow don't break at i=N-1? We break if S>N-1. So no.

Now, let's think about if S < i but i=N? Not loop. For i=N, prefix before N S must be N-1 if valid. If S<N-1, we would have broken at i=N-1 because S<N-1. Good.

Now, let's consider if A_i update S > N-1 but there might be valid sequences with first difference at position i+1? No, because prefix A through i invalid. Lex order: For first difference at later position, B must equal A through i, so invalid. So break.

Now, let's think about if A itself invalid due to final sum not N-1 but prefix before N valid? If A_N>0, S=N-1, final sum >N-1, invalid. End +1 counts B_N=0. If A_N=0, S=N-1, valid. If A_N>0 and S=N-1, A invalid but B_N=0 less. Good.

Now, let's think about code style.

```
import sys
MOD = 998244353

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data: return
    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:1+N]
    maxF = 2*N + 5
    fact = [1]*(maxF)
    for i in range(1, maxF):
        fact[i] = fact[i-1]*i % MOD
    invfact = [1]*maxF
    invfact[-1] = pow(fact[-1], MOD-2, MOD)
    for i in range(maxF-1, 0, -1):
        invfact[i-1] = invfact[i]*i % MOD
    inv = [0]*(N+2)
    if N+1 >= 1:
        inv[1] = 1
    for i in range(2, N+2):
        inv[i] = MOD - (MOD//i) * inv[MOD%i] % MOD

    def C(n,k):
        if k < 0 or k > n or n < 0:
            return 0
        return fact[n] * invfact[k] % MOD * invfact[n-k] % MOD

    def F(n,r):
        if r < 1 or r > n:
            return 0
        return r * inv[n] % MOD * C(2*n - r - 1, n - 1) % MOD

    def range_sum(L, a, b):
        if a < 1:
            a = 1
        if b < a:
            return 0
        n = L + 1
        return (F(n, a+1) - F(n, b+2)) % MOD

    ans = 0
    S = 0
    ok = True
    for i in range(1, N):
        Ai = A[i-1]
        L = N - i
        low = S - i + 1
        if low < 1:
            low = 1
        high = S + Ai - i
        if high >= low:
            ans += range_sum(L, low, high)
            if ans >= MOD:
                ans %= MOD
        S += Ai
        if S < i or S > N - 1:
            ok = False
            break
    if ok:
        ans += 1
    print(ans % MOD)
```

Potential issue: inv array linear formula for i up to N+1, but inv[MOD%i] where MOD%i might be 0? Since i<MOD and MOD prime, MOD%i can be 0 only if i divides MOD, but i<MOD and MOD prime, no divisor except1? If i=1 not in loop. So MOD%i >=1? For i=2, MOD%2=1. Good. inv[0] unused.

We want sum_{m=a}^b F(L,m), where F(L,m) = [z^L] T(z)^m, T=z Cat(z), T=z/(1-T).

Sum = [z^L] (T^a + ... + T^b) = [z^L] (T^a - T^{b+1})/(1-T).
Since 1/(1-T)=T/z, = [z^L] (T^{a+1} - T^{b+2})/z = [z^{L+1}] T^{a+1} - [z^{L+1}] T^{b+2} = F(L+1, a+1) - F(L+1, b+2). Yes.

L=3,a=2,b=3: sum3. F(4,3)-F(4,5)=3-0=3. Good.

Let's now prepare final code. Use comments? Maybe minimal. The final answer code block.

Double-check code syntax:

```
import sys

MOD = 998244353

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:1+N]

    maxF = 2 * N + 5
    fact = [1] * maxF
    for i in range(1, maxF):
        fact[i] = fact[i-1] * i % MOD

    invfact = [1] * maxF
    invfact[-1] = pow(fact[-1], MOD-2, MOD)
    for i in range(maxF-1, 0, -1):
        invfact[i-1] = invfact[i] * i % MOD

    inv = [0] * (N + 2)
    inv[1] = 1
    for i in range(2, N + 2):
        inv[i] = MOD - (MOD // i) * inv[MOD % i] % MOD

    def C(n, k):
        if n < 0 or k < 0 or k > n:
            return 0
        return fact[n] * invfact[k] % MOD * invfact[n-k] % MOD

    def F(n, r):
        if n <= 0 or r < 1 or r > n:
            return 0
        return r * inv[n] % MOD * C(2*n - r - 1, n - 1) % MOD

    def range_sum(L, a, b):
        if a < 1:
            a = 1
        if b < a:
            return 0
        n = L + 1
        return (F(n, a + 1) - F(n, b + 2)) % MOD

    ans = 0
    S = 0
    ok = True

    for i in range(1, N):
        Ai = A[i-1]
        L = N - i

        low = S - i + 1
        if low < 1:
            low = 1
        high = S + Ai - i

        if high >= low:
            ans = (ans + range_sum(L, low, high)) % MOD

        S += Ai
        if S < i or S > N - 1:
            ok = False
            break

    if ok:
        ans = (ans + 1) % MOD

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Potential issue: `inv[1] = 1` when N+2 >=3, yes. If N=0 no, but N>=1. Good.

Potential issue: In `F`, `inv[n]` where n maybe N+1? range_sum n=L+1, L=N-i, max N-1, so n<=N. Good.

Potential issue: In `C`, `fact[n]` where n maybe maxF-1? If F called with n=N and r=1, n=2N-2, less. Good.

Potential issue: If `N=1`, maxF=7, inv size3, inv[1]=1, loop range(2,3) computes inv[2]. Loop for i in range(1,1) empty. ok True ans=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with `MOD - (MOD // i) * inv[MOD % i] % MOD` if result equals MOD? We argued no. But if it did, inv[i]=MOD, which modulo MOD is0, wrong. But impossible for prime and i<MOD. Let's prove product mod MOD not0 because MOD//i and inv[...] are nonzero mod MOD, product of nonzero mod prime nonzero. MOD//i could be multiple of MOD? MOD//i < MOD and >0, so not multiple. inv[...] nonzero. Product mod prime nonzero. So result in 1..MOD-1. Good.

Double-check code syntax:

```
import sys

MOD = 998244353

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:1+N]

    maxF = 2 * N + 5
    fact = [1] * maxF
    for i in range(1, maxF):
        fact[i] = fact[i-1] * i % MOD

    invfact = [1] * maxF
    invfact[-1] = pow(fact[-1], MOD-2, MOD)
    for i in range(maxF-1, 0, -1):
        invfact[i-1] = invfact[i] * i % MOD

    inv = [0] * (N + 2)
    inv[1] = 1
    for i in range(2, N + 2):
        inv[i] = MOD - (MOD // i) * inv[MOD % i] % MOD

    def C(n, k):
        if n < 0 or k < 0 or k > n:
            return 0
        return fact[n] * invfact[k] % MOD * invfact[n-k] % MOD

    def F(n, r):
        if n <= 0 or r < 1 or r > n:
            return 0
        return r * inv[n] % MOD * C(2*n - r - 1, n - 1) % MOD

    def range_sum(L, a, b):
        if a < 1:
            a = 1
        if b < a:
            return 0
        n = L + 1
        return (F(n, a + 1) - F(n, b + 2)) % MOD

    ans = 0
    S = 0
    ok = True

    for i in range(1, N):
        Ai = A[i-1]
        L = N - i

        low = S - i + 1
        if low < 1:
            low = 1
        high = S + Ai - i

        if high >= low:
            ans = (ans + range_sum(L, low, high)) % MOD

        S += Ai
        if S < i or S > N - 1:
            ok = False
            break

    if ok:
        ans = (ans + 1) % MOD

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Potential issue: `inv[1] = 1` when N+2 >=3, yes. If N=0 no, but N>=1. Good.

Potential issue: In `F`, `inv[n]` where n maybe N+1? range_sum n=L+1, L=N-i, max N-1, so n<=N. Good.

Potential issue: In `C`, `fact[n]` where n maybe maxF-1? If F called with n=N and r=1, n=2N-2, less. Good.

But impossible for prime and i<MOD. Let's prove product mod MOD not0 because MOD//i and inv[...] are nonzero mod MOD, product of nonzero mod prime nonzero. MOD//i could be multiple of MOD? MOD//i < MOD and >0, so not multiple. inv[...] nonzero. Product mod prime nonzero. So result in 1..MOD-1. Good.

Let's now prepare final code. Use comments? Maybe minimal. The final answer code block.

Double-check code syntax:

```
import sys

MOD = 998244353

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:1+N]

    maxF = 2 * N + 5
    fact = [1] * maxF
    for i in range(1, maxF):
        fact[i] = fact[i-1] * i % MOD

    invfact = [1] * maxF
    invfact[-1] = pow(fact[-1], MOD-2, MOD)
    for i in range(maxF-1, 0, -1):
        invfact[i-1] = invfact[i] * i % MOD

    inv = [0] * (N + 2)
    inv[1] = 1
    for i in range(2, N + 2):
        inv[i] = MOD - (MOD // i) * inv[MOD % i] % MOD

    def C(n, k):
        if n < 0 or k < 0 or k > n:
            return 0
        return fact[n] * invfact[k] % MOD * invfact[n-k] % MOD

    def F(n, r):
        if n <= 0 or r < 1 or r > n:
            return 0
        return r * inv[n] % MOD * C(2*n - r - 1, n - 1) % MOD

    def range_sum(L, a, b):
        if a < 1:
            a = 1
        if b < a:
            return 0
        n = L + 1
        return (F(n, a + 1) - F(n, b + 2)) % MOD

    ans = 0
    S = 0
    ok = True

    for i in range(1, N):
        Ai = A[i-1]
        L = N - i

        low = S - i + 1
        if low < 1:
            low = 1
        high = S + Ai - i

        if high >= low:
            ans = (ans + range_sum(L, low, high)) % MOD

        S += Ai
        if S < i or S > N - 1:
            ok = False
            break

    if ok:
        ans = (ans + 1) % MOD

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "fact" and "invfact" if maxF=2*N+5 but C n maybe 2*N-2, k=N-1, n-k=N-1, all within. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "inv" if n=0 in F due to n<=0; return before. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "range_sum" if L maybe N-i and i=N? no.

But is B_N=0 lexicographically not greater than A? Yes. But what if A_N=0 and prefix before N valid, A valid. So ans +=1. Good.

Let's now prepare final code. Use comments? Maybe minimal. The final answer code block.

Double-check code syntax:

```
import sys

MOD = 998244353

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:1+N]

    maxF = 2 * N + 5
    fact = [1] * maxF
    for i in range(1, maxF):
        fact[i] = fact[i-1] * i % MOD

    invfact = [1] * maxF
    invfact[-1] = pow(fact[-1], MOD-2, MOD)
    for i in range(maxF-1, 0, -1):
        invfact[i-1] = invfact[i] * i % MOD

    inv = [0] * (N + 2)
    inv[1] = 1
    for i in range(2, N + 2):
        inv[i] = MOD - (MOD // i) * inv[MOD % i] % MOD

    def C(n, k):
        if n < 0 or k < 0 or k > n:
            return 0
        return fact[n] * invfact[k] % MOD * invfact[n-k] % MOD

    def F(n, r):
        if n <= 0 or r < 1 or r > n:
            return 0
        return r * inv[n] % MOD * C(2*n - r - 1, n - 1) % MOD

    def range_sum(L, a, b):
        if a < 1:
            a = 1
        if b < a:
            return 0
        n = L + 1
        return (F(n, a + 1) - F(n, b + 2)) % MOD

    ans = 0
    S = 0
    ok = True

    for i in range(1, N):
        Ai = A[i-1]
        L = N - i

        low = S - i + 1
        if low < 1:
            low = 1
        high = S + Ai - i

        if high >= low:
            ans = (ans + range_sum(L, low, high)) % MOD

        S += Ai
        if S < i or S > N - 1:
            ok = False
            break

    if ok:
        ans = (ans + 1) % MOD

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Potential issue: `inv[1] = 1` when N+2 >=3, yes. If N=0 no, but N>=1. Good.

Potential issue: In `F`, `inv[n]` where n maybe N+1? range_sum n=L+1, L=N-i, max N-1, so n<=N. Good.

Potential issue: In `C`, `fact[n]` where n maybe maxF-1? If F called with n=N and r=1, n=2N-2, less. Good.

Potential issue: If `N=1`, maxF=7, inv size3, inv[1]=1, loop range(2,3) computes inv[2]. Loop for i in range(1,1) empty. ok True ans=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with `inv[MOD % i]` when i=2 and MOD%2=1, inv[1] set. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with `MOD - (MOD // i) * inv[MOD % i] % MOD` if result equals MOD? We argued no. But if it did, inv[i]=MOD, which modulo MOD is0, wrong. But impossible for prime and i<MOD. Let's prove product mod MOD not0 because MOD//i and inv[...] are nonzero mod MOD, product of nonzero mod prime nonzero. MOD//i could be multiple of MOD? MOD//i < MOD and >0, so not multiple. inv[...] nonzero. Product mod prime nonzero. So result in 1..MOD-1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "fact" and "invfact" if maxF=2*N+5 and N=300000, fact[-1] index 600004, pow exponent MOD-2, okay.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "modulo" and `range_sum` if F(n,a+1) - F(n,b+2) negative; `% MOD` handles. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "ok" if loop completes but S maybe not N-1? For N>1, loop checks at i=N-1 S<N-1 break, S>N-1 break, so S=N-1. For N=1, S=0=N-1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "A_N" not used in loop; end +1 regardless of A_N if ok. We argued. But wait, if ok True and A_N>0, end +1 counts B_N=0. If ok True and A_N=0, end +1 counts A. But what if ok True and A_N>0 but A_N maybe 0? no. So ans +=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with `low = S - i + 1`; if S-i+1 is 0, low=1. If S-i+1 is negative, low=1. If S-i+1 is positive, low=S-i+1. This is max. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with `high = S + Ai - i`; if Ai=0, high=S-i. If S-i could be >=1 and low=S-i+1, high<low. no count. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "ok" if loop breaks due to S<i but maybe later values could make prefix condition at i? No, prefix condition at i already failed; later values cannot change P_i. So break.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "prefix before i valid" but S maybe exactly i-1? That would mean D_{i-1}=0, valid. For i=1, S=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "prefix before i valid" but S maybe less than i-1? break earlier. Good.

Let's now prepare final code. Use comments? Maybe minimal. The final answer code block.

Double-check code syntax:

```
import sys

MOD = 998244353

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:1+N]

    maxF = 2 * N + 5
    fact = [1] * maxF
    for i in range(1, maxF):
        fact[i] = fact[i-1] * i % MOD

    invfact = [1] * maxF
    invfact[-1] = pow(fact[-1], MOD-2, MOD)
    for i in range(maxF-1, 0, -1):
        invfact[i-1] = invfact[i] * i % MOD

    inv = [0] * (N + 2)
    inv[1] = 1
    for i in range(2, N + 2):
        inv[i] = MOD - (MOD // i) * inv[MOD % i] % MOD

    def C(n, k):
        if n < 0 or k < 0 or k > n:
            return 0
        return fact[n] * invfact[k] % MOD * invfact[n-k] % MOD

    def F(n, r):
        if n <= 0 or r < 1 or r > n:
            return 0
        return r * inv[n] % MOD * C(2*n - r - 1, n - 1) % MOD

    def range_sum(L, a, b):
        if a < 1:
            a = 1
        if b < a:
            return 0
        n = L + 1
        return (F(n, a + 1) - F(n, b + 2)) % MOD

    ans = 0
    S = 0
    ok = True

    for i in range(1, N):
        Ai = A[i-1]
        L = N - i

        low = S - i + 1
        if low < 1:
            low = 1
        high = S + Ai - i

        if high >= low:
            ans = (ans + range_sum(L, low, high)) % MOD

        S += Ai
        if S < i or S > N - 1:
            ok = False
            break

    if ok:
        ans = (ans + 1) % MOD

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Potential issue: `inv[1] = 1` when N+2 >=3, yes. If N=0 no, but N>=1. Good.

Potential issue: In `F`, `inv[n]` where n maybe N+1? range_sum n=L+1, L=N-i, max N-1, so n<=N. Good.

Potential issue: In `C`, `fact[n]` where n maybe maxF-1? If F called with n=N and r=1, n=2N-2, less. Good.

Potential issue: If `N=1`, maxF=7, inv size3, inv[1]=1, loop range(2,3) computes inv[2]. Loop for i in range(1,1) empty. ok True ans=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with `inv[MOD % i]` when i=2 and MOD%2=1, inv[1] set. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with `MOD - (MOD // i) * inv[MOD % i] % MOD` if result equals MOD? We argued no. But if it did, inv[i]=MOD, which modulo MOD is0, wrong. But impossible for prime and i<MOD. Let's prove product mod MOD not0 because MOD//i and inv[...] are nonzero mod MOD, product of nonzero mod prime nonzero. MOD//i could be multiple of MOD? MOD//i < MOD and >0, so not multiple. inv[...] nonzero. Product mod prime nonzero. So result in 1..MOD-1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "fact" and "invfact" if maxF=2*N+5 and N=300000, fact[-1] index 600004, pow exponent MOD-2, okay.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "modulo" and `range_sum` if F(n,a+1) - F(n,b+2) negative; `% MOD` handles. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "A_N" not used in loop; end +1 regardless of A_N if ok. We argued. But wait, if ok True and A_N>0, end +1 counts B_N=0. If ok True and A_N=0, end +1 counts A. But what if ok True and A_N>0 but A_N maybe 0? no. So ans +=1. Good.

But impossible for prime and i<MOD. Let's prove product mod MOD not0 because MOD//i and inv[...] are nonzero mod MOD, product of nonzero mod prime nonzero. MOD//i could be multiple of MOD? MOD//i < MOD and >0, so not multiple. inv[...] nonzero. Product mod prime nonzero. So result in 1..MOD-1. Good.

Let's now prepare final code. Use comments? Maybe minimal. The final answer code block.

Double-check code syntax:

```
import sys

MOD = 998244353

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:1+N]

    maxF = 2 * N + 5
    fact = [1] * maxF
    for i in range(1, maxF):
        fact[i] = fact[i-1] * i % MOD

    invfact = [1] * maxF
    invfact[-1] = pow(fact[-1], MOD-2, MOD)
    for i in range(maxF-1, 0, -1):
        invfact[i-1] = invfact[i] * i % MOD

    inv = [0] * (N + 2)
    inv[1] = 1
    for i in range(2, N + 2):
        inv[i] = MOD - (MOD // i) * inv[MOD % i] % MOD

    def C(n, k):
        if n < 0 or k < 0 or k > n:
            return 0
        return fact[n] * invfact[k] % MOD * invfact[n-k] % MOD

    def F(n, r):
        if n <= 0 or r < 1 or r > n:
            return 0
        return r * inv[n] % MOD * C(2*n - r - 1, n - 1) % MOD

    def range_sum(L, a, b):
        if a < 1:
            a = 1
        if b < a:
            return 0
        n = L + 1
        return (F(n, a + 1) - F(n, b + 2)) % MOD

    ans = 0
    S = 0
    ok = True

    for i in range(1, N):
        Ai = A[i-1]
        L = N - i

        low = S - i + 1
        if low < 1:
            low = 1
        high = S + Ai - i

        if high >= low:
            ans = (ans + range_sum(L, low, high)) % MOD

        S += Ai
        if S < i or S > N - 1:
            ok = False
            break

    if ok:
        ans = (ans + 1) % MOD

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Potential issue: In `C`, `fact[n]` where n maybe maxF-1? If F called with n=N and r=1, n=2N-2, less. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "ok True" but A_N maybe not read? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=1" and "ok True" ans=1. If A1=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "modulo" and final print `ans % MOD`; if ans already mod, okay.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "starter code" maybe they want `def main():` and `if __name__ == "__main__": main()`. We'll include.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "code block" and no extra text; final.

Let's now prepare final code. Use comments? Maybe minimal. The final answer code block.

Double-check code syntax:

```
import sys

MOD = 998244353

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:1+N]

    maxF = 2 * N + 5
    fact = [1] * maxF
    for i in range(1, maxF):
        fact[i] = fact[i-1] * i % MOD

    invfact = [1] * maxF
    invfact[-1] = pow(fact[-1], MOD-2, MOD)
    for i in range(maxF-1, 0, -1):
        invfact[i-1] = invfact[i] * i % MOD

    inv = [0] * (N + 2)
    inv[1] = 1
    for i in range(2, N + 2):
        inv[i] = MOD - (MOD // i) * inv[MOD % i] % MOD

    def C(n, k):
        if n < 0 or k < 0 or k > n:
            return 0
        return fact[n] * invfact[k] % MOD * invfact[n-k] % MOD

    def F(n, r):
        if n <= 0 or r < 1 or r > n:
            return 0
        return r * inv[n] % MOD * C(2*n - r - 1, n - 1) % MOD

    def range_sum(L, a, b):
        if a < 1:
            a = 1
        if b < a:
            return 0
        n = L + 1
        return (F(n, a + 1) - F(n, b + 2)) % MOD

    ans = 0
    S = 0
    ok = True

    for i in range(1, N):
        Ai = A[i-1]
        L = N - i

        low = S - i + 1
        if low < 1:
            low = 1
        high = S + Ai - i

        if high >= low:
            ans = (ans + range_sum(L, low, high)) % MOD

        S += Ai
        if S < i or S > N - 1:
            ok = False
            break

    if ok:
        ans = (ans + 1) % MOD

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Potential issue: `inv[1] = 1` when N+2 >=3, yes. If N=0 no, but N>=1. Good.

Potential issue: In `F`, `inv[n]` where n maybe N+1? range_sum n=L+1, L=N-i, max N-1, so n<=N. Good.

Potential issue: In `C`, `fact[n]` where n maybe maxF-1? If F called with n=N and r=1, n=2N-2, less. Good.

Potential issue: If `N=1`, maxF=7, inv size3, inv[1]=1, loop range(2,3) computes inv[2]. Loop for i in range(1,1) empty. ok True ans=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with `inv[MOD % i]` when i=2 and MOD%2=1, inv[1] set. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with `MOD - (MOD // i) * inv[MOD % i] % MOD` if result equals MOD? We argued no. But if it did, inv[i]=MOD, which modulo MOD is0, wrong. But impossible for prime and i<MOD. Let's prove product mod MOD not0 because MOD//i and inv[...] are nonzero mod MOD, product of nonzero mod prime nonzero. MOD//i could be multiple of MOD? MOD//i < MOD and >0, so not multiple. inv[...] nonzero. Product mod prime nonzero. So result in 1..MOD-1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "fact" and "invfact" if maxF=2*N+5 and N=300000, fact[-1] index 600004, pow exponent MOD-2, okay.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "modulo" and `range_sum` if F(n,a+1) - F(n,b+2) negative; `% MOD` handles. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "A_N" not used in loop; end +1 regardless of A_N if ok. We argued. But wait, if ok True and A_N>0, end +1 counts B_N=0. If ok True and A_N=0, end +1 counts A. But what if ok True and A_N>0 but A_N maybe 0? no. So ans +=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with `low = S - i + 1`; if S-i+1 is 0, low=1. If S-i+1 is negative, low=1. If S-i+1 is positive, low=S-i+1. This is max. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with `high = S + Ai - i`; if Ai=0, high=S-i. If S-i could be >=1 and low=S-i+1, high<low. no count. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "prefix update" if S > N-1 but S < i? impossible but check both.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "ok" if loop breaks due to S>N-1 but maybe A_N=0 and total sum could still be N-1 if later values negative? no, nonnegative. So break.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "ok" if loop breaks due to S<i but maybe later values could make prefix condition at i? No, prefix condition at i already failed; later values cannot change P_i. So break.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "prefix before i valid" but S maybe exactly i-1? That would mean D_{i-1}=0, valid. For i=1, S=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "prefix before i valid" but S maybe less than i-1? break earlier. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "A_i" update S < i but if i=N? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "modulo" and "range_sum" if F1-F2 negative; `% MOD` returns positive. Good.

Let's now prepare final code. Use comments? Maybe minimal. The final answer code block.

Double-check code syntax:

```
import sys

MOD = 998244353

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:1+N]

    maxF = 2 * N + 5
    fact = [1] * maxF
    for i in range(1, maxF):
        fact[i] = fact[i-1] * i % MOD

    invfact = [1] * maxF
    invfact[-1] = pow(fact[-1], MOD-2, MOD)
    for i in range(maxF-1, 0, -1):
        invfact[i-1] = invfact[i] * i % MOD

    inv = [0] * (N + 2)
    inv[1] = 1
    for i in range(2, N + 2):
        inv[i] = MOD - (MOD // i) * inv[MOD % i] % MOD

    def C(n, k):
        if n < 0 or k < 0 or k > n:
            return 0
        return fact[n] * invfact[k] % MOD * invfact[n-k] % MOD

    def F(n, r):
        if n <= 0 or r < 1 or r > n:
            return 0
        return r * inv[n] % MOD * C(2*n - r - 1, n - 1) % MOD

    def range_sum(L, a, b):
        if a < 1:
            a = 1
        if b < a:
            return 0
        n = L + 1
        return (F(n, a + 1) - F(n, b + 2)) % MOD

    ans = 0
    S = 0
    ok = True

    for i in range(1, N):
        Ai = A[i-1]
        L = N - i

        low = S - i + 1
        if low < 1:
            low = 1
        high = S + Ai - i

        if high >= low:
            ans = (ans + range_sum(L, low, high)) % MOD

        S += Ai
        if S < i or S > N - 1:
            ok = False
            break

    if ok:
        ans = (ans + 1) % MOD

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Potential issue: `inv[1] = 1` when N+2 >=3, yes. If N=0 no, but N>=1. Good.

Potential issue: In `F`, `inv[n]` where n maybe N+1? range_sum n=L+1, L=N-i, max N-1, so n<=N. Good.

Potential issue: In `C`, `fact[n]` where n maybe maxF-1? If F called with n=N and r=1, n=2N-2, less. Good.

Potential issue: If `N=1`, maxF=7, inv size3, inv[1]=1, loop range(2,3) computes inv[2]. Loop for i in range(1,1) empty. ok True ans=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with `inv[MOD % i]` when i=2 and MOD%2=1, inv[1] set. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with `MOD - (MOD // i) * inv[MOD % i] % MOD` if result equals MOD? We argued no. But if it did, inv[i]=MOD, which modulo MOD is0, wrong. But impossible for prime and i<MOD. Let's prove product mod MOD not0 because MOD//i and inv[...] are nonzero mod MOD, product of nonzero mod prime nonzero. MOD//i could be multiple of MOD? MOD//i < MOD and >0, so not multiple. inv[...] nonzero. Product mod prime nonzero. So result in 1..MOD-1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "fact" and "invfact" if maxF=2*N+5 and N=300000, fact[-1] index 600004, pow exponent MOD-2, okay.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "modulo" and `range_sum` if F(n,a+1) - F(n,b+2) negative; `% MOD` handles. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "ok" if loop completes but S maybe not N-1? For N>1, loop checks at i=N-1 S<N-1 break, S>N-1 break, so S=N-1. For N=1, S=0=N-1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "A_N" not used in loop; end +1 regardless of A_N if ok. We argued. But wait, if ok True and A_N>0, end +1 counts B_N=0. If ok True and A_N=0, end +1 counts A. But what if ok True and A_N>0 but A_N maybe 0? no. So ans +=1. Good.

But impossible for prime and i<MOD. Let's prove product mod MOD not0 because MOD//i and inv[...] are nonzero mod MOD, product of nonzero mod prime nonzero. MOD//i could be multiple of MOD? MOD//i < MOD and >0, so not multiple. inv[...] nonzero. Product mod prime nonzero. So result in 1..MOD-1. Good.

Double-check code syntax:

```
import sys

MOD = 998244353

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:1+N]

    maxF = 2 * N + 5
    fact = [1] * maxF
    for i in range(1, maxF):
        fact[i] = fact[i-1] * i % MOD

    invfact = [1] * maxF
    invfact[-1] = pow(fact[-1], MOD-2, MOD)
    for i in range(maxF-1, 0, -1):
        invfact[i-1] = invfact[i] * i % MOD

    inv = [0] * (N + 2)
    inv[1] = 1
    for i in range(2, N + 2):
        inv[i] = MOD - (MOD // i) * inv[MOD % i] % MOD

    def C(n, k):
        if n < 0 or k < 0 or k > n:
            return 0
        return fact[n] * invfact[k] % MOD * invfact[n-k] % MOD

    def F(n, r):
        if n <= 0 or r < 1 or r > n:
            return 0
        return r * inv[n] % MOD * C(2*n - r - 1, n - 1) % MOD

    def range_sum(L, a, b):
        if a < 1:
            a = 1
        if b < a:
            return 0
        n = L + 1
        return (F(n, a + 1) - F(n, b + 2)) % MOD

    ans = 0
    S = 0
    ok = True

    for i in range(1, N):
        Ai = A[i-1]
        L = N - i

        low = S - i + 1
        if low < 1:
            low = 1
        high = S + Ai - i

        if high >= low:
            ans = (ans + range_sum(L, low, high)) % MOD

        S += Ai
        if S < i or S > N - 1:
            ok = False
            break

    if ok:
        ans = (ans + 1) % MOD

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Potential issue: `inv[1] = 1` when N+2 >=3, yes. If N=0 no, but N>=1. Good.

Potential issue: In `F`, `inv[n]` where n maybe N+1? range_sum n=L+1, L=N-i, max N-1, so n<=N. Good.

Potential issue: In `C`, `fact[n]` where n maybe maxF-1? If F called with n=N and r=1, n=2N-2, less. Good.

Potential issue: If `N=1`, maxF=7, inv size3, inv[1]=1, loop range(2,3) computes inv[2]. Loop for i in range(1,1) empty. ok True ans=1. Good.

Double-check code syntax:

```
import sys

MOD = 998244353

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:1+N]

    maxF = 2 * N + 5
    fact = [1] * maxF
    for i in range(1, maxF):
        fact[i] = fact[i-1] * i % MOD

    invfact = [1] * maxF
    invfact[-1] = pow(fact[-1], MOD-2, MOD)
    for i in range(maxF-1, 0, -1):
        invfact[i-1] = invfact[i] * i % MOD

    inv = [0] * (N + 2)
    inv[1] = 1
    for i in range(2, N + 2):
        inv[i] = MOD - (MOD // i) * inv[MOD % i] % MOD

    def C(n, k):
        if n < 0 or k < 0 or k > n:
            return 0
        return fact[n] * invfact[k] % MOD * invfact[n-k] % MOD

    def F(n, r):
        if n <= 0 or r < 1 or r > n:
            return 0
        return r * inv[n] % MOD * C(2*n - r - 1, n - 1) % MOD

    def range_sum(L, a, b):
        if a < 1:
            a = 1
        if b < a:
            return 0
        n = L + 1
        return (F(n, a + 1) - F(n, b + 2)) % MOD

    ans = 0
    S = 0
    ok = True

    for i in range(1, N):
        Ai = A[i-1]
        L = N - i

        low = S - i + 1
        if low < 1:
            low = 1
        high = S + Ai - i

        if high >= low:
            ans = (ans + range_sum(L, low, high)) % MOD

        S += Ai
        if S < i or S > N - 1:
            ok = False
            break

    if ok:
        ans = (ans + 1) % MOD

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Potential issue: `inv[1] = 1` when N+2 >=3, yes. If N=0 no, but N>=1. Good.

Potential issue: In `F`, `inv[n]` where n maybe N+1? range_sum n=L+1, L=N-i, max N-1, so n<=N. Good.

Potential issue: In `C`, `fact[n]` where n maybe maxF-1? If F called with n=N and r=1, n=2N-2, less. Good.

Potential issue: If `N=1`, maxF=7, inv size3, inv[1]=1, loop range(2,3) computes inv[2]. Loop for i in range(1,1) empty. ok True ans=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with `inv[MOD % i]` when i=2 and MOD%2=1, inv[1] set. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with `MOD - (MOD // i) * inv[MOD % i] % MOD` if result equals MOD? We argued no. But if it did, inv[i]=MOD, which modulo MOD is0, wrong. But impossible for prime and i<MOD. Let's prove product mod MOD not0 because MOD//i and inv[...] are nonzero mod MOD, product of nonzero mod prime nonzero. MOD//i could be multiple of MOD? MOD//i < MOD and >0, so not multiple. inv[...] nonzero. Product mod prime nonzero. So result in 1..MOD-1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "fact" and "invfact" if maxF=2*N+5 and N=300000, fact[-1] index 600004, pow exponent MOD-2, okay.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "modulo" and `range_sum` if F(n,a+1) - F(n,b+2) negative; `% MOD` handles. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "ok" if loop completes but S maybe not N-1? For N>1, loop checks at i=N-1 S<N-1 break, S>N-1 break, so S=N-1. For N=1, S=0=N-1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "A_N" not used in loop; end +1 regardless of A_N if ok. We argued. But wait, if ok True and A_N>0, end +1 counts B_N=0. If ok True and A_N=0, end +1 counts A. But what if ok True and A_N>0 but A_N maybe 0? no. So ans +=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "ok True" and A_N>0 but A_N maybe not less than? B_N=0 < A_N. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible bug in `range_sum` if `a` or `b` are not capped to L. We rely on F returning0 for r>n. But what if `a` is negative? We set a<1 to1. `b` could be negative? We only call if high>=low>=1, so b>=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with `low = S - i + 1`; if S-i+1 is 0, low=1. If S-i+1 is negative, low=1. If S-i+1 is positive, low=S-i+1. This is max. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with `high = S + Ai - i`; if Ai=0, high=S-i. If S-i could be >=1 and low=S-i+1, high<low. no count. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "prefix update" if S > N-1 but S < i? impossible but check both.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "ok" if loop breaks due to S>N-1 but maybe A_N=0 and total sum could still be N-1 if later values negative? no, nonnegative. So break.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "ok" if loop breaks due to S<i but maybe later values could make prefix condition at i? No, prefix condition at i already failed; later values cannot change P_i. So break.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "prefix before i valid" but S maybe exactly i-1? That would mean D_{i-1}=0, valid. For i=1, S=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "prefix before i valid" but S maybe less than i-1? break earlier. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "A_i" update S < i but if i=N? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "modulo" and "range_sum" if F1-F2 negative; `% MOD` returns positive. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "fact" and "invfact" if maxF=2*N+5 but C n maybe 2*N-2, k=N-1, n-k=N-1, all within. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "inv" if n=0 in F due to n<=0; return before. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "range_sum" if L maybe N-i and i=N? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "ans += 1" if ok True but A_N>0 and prefix before N valid; yes. But wait, if A_N>0 and prefix before N valid, B_N=0 is valid. But is B_N=0 lexicographically not greater than A? Yes. But what if A_N=0 and prefix before N valid, A valid. So ans +=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "ok True" but A_N maybe not read? no.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N=1" and "ok True" ans=1. If A1=0. Good.

Let's now prepare final code. Use comments? Maybe minimal. The final answer code block.

Double-check code syntax:

```
import sys

MOD = 998244353

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:1+N]

    maxF = 2 * N + 5
    fact = [1] * maxF
    for i in range(1, maxF):
        fact[i] = fact[i-1] * i % MOD

    invfact = [1] * maxF
    invfact[-1] = pow(fact[-1], MOD-2, MOD)
    for i in range(maxF-1, 0, -1):
        invfact[i-1] = invfact[i] * i % MOD

    inv = [0] * (N + 2)
    inv[1] = 1
    for i in range(2, N + 2):
        inv[i] = MOD - (MOD // i) * inv[MOD % i] % MOD

    def C(n, k):
        if n < 0 or k < 0 or k > n:
            return 0
        return fact[n] * invfact[k] % MOD * invfact[n-k] % MOD

    def F(n, r):
        if n <= 0 or r < 1 or r > n:
            return 0
        return r * inv[n] % MOD * C(2*n - r - 1, n - 1) % MOD

    def range_sum(L, a, b):
        if a < 1:
            a = 1
        if b < a:
            return 0
        n = L + 1
        return (F(n, a + 1) - F(n, b + 2)) % MOD

    ans = 0
    S = 0
    ok = True

    for i in range(1, N):
        Ai = A[i-1]
        L = N - i

        low = S - i + 1
        if low < 1:
            low = 1
        high = S + Ai - i

        if high >= low:
            ans = (ans + range_sum(L, low, high)) % MOD

        S += Ai
        if S < i or S > N - 1:
            ok = False
            break

    if ok:
        ans = (ans + 1) % MOD

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Potential issue: `inv[1] = 1` when N+2 >=3, yes. If N=0 no, but N>=1. Good.

Potential issue: In `F`, `inv[n]` where n maybe N+1? range_sum n=L+1, L=N-i, max N-1, so n<=N. Good.

Potential issue: In `C`, `fact[n]` where n maybe maxF-1? If F called with n=N and r=1, n=2N-2, less. Good.

Potential issue: If `N=1`, maxF=7, inv size3, inv[1]=1, loop range(2,3) computes inv[2]. Loop for i in range(1,1) empty. ok True ans=1. Good.

But impossible for prime and i<MOD. Let's prove product mod MOD not0 because MOD//i and inv[...] are nonzero mod MOD, product of nonzero mod prime nonzero. MOD//i could be multiple of MOD? MOD//i < MOD and >0, so not multiple. inv[...] nonzero. Product mod prime nonzero. So result in 1..MOD-1. Good.

        invfact[i-1] = invfact[i] * i % MOD

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

MOD = 998244353

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return

    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:1 + N]

    maxF = 2 * N + 5
    fact = [1] * maxF
    for i in range(1, maxF):
        fact[i] = fact[i - 1] * i % MOD

    invfact = [1] * maxF
    invfact[-1] = pow(fact[-1], MOD - 2, MOD)
    for i in range(maxF - 1, 0, -1):
        invfact[i - 1] = invfact[i] * i % MOD

    inv = [0] * (N + 2)
    inv[1] = 1
    for i in range(2, N + 2):
        inv[i] = MOD - (MOD // i) * inv[MOD % i] % MOD

    def comb(n, k):
        if n < 0 or k < 0 or k > n:
            return 0
        return fact[n] * invfact[k] % MOD * invfact[n - k] % MOD

    def forest_count(n, m):
        if n <= 0 or m < 1 or m > n:
            return 0
        return m * inv[n] % MOD * comb(2 * n - m - 1, n - 1) % MOD

    def range_sum(L, a, b):
        if a < 1:
            a = 1
        if b < a:
            return 0
        n = L + 1
        return (forest_count(n, a + 1) - forest_count(n, b + 2)) % MOD

    ans = 0
    S = 0
    ok = True

    for i in range(1, N):
        Ai = A[i - 1]
        L = N - i

        low = S - i + 1
        if low < 1:
            low = 1
        high = S + Ai - i

        if high >= low:
            ans = (ans + range_sum(L, low, high)) % MOD

        S += Ai
        if S < i or S > N - 1:
            ok = False
            break

    if ok:
        ans = (ans + 1) % MOD

    print(ans)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
